| R379 | `issue #191` | `systems/combat.md` | 6.3, the party-member row of the death-branch table | "Character HP forced to zero, roster status byte set to `'D'`, marked-dead bit ORed in, **death audio played**, active-player sentinel set to `0xFF` if the dead character was active". | Two withdrawals in one cell. **There is no party-death cue.** The arm makes exactly one call - the full stats-panel redraw - and no speaker primitive is reachable from it, so nothing sounds before or after the corpse tile or the `'D'` write; Section 11.1's Target-dies row ("no cue of its own") and `systems/audio.md` were the correct ones, and audio.md section 9 now lists this among its silence boundaries. What is audible around a party death belongs to the blow: the swing sweep and the impact burst, both already played before the first of these writes. Separately, the shared world tick's ambient tile-audio pass has an in-arena branch and can sound a scenery tone near a death, keyed on nearby tiles rather than on the death. **And the two status writes were published in the wrong order:** the marked-dead bit precedes the `'D'` roster letter, not the reverse. The full order is HP to zero, marked-dead bit, `'D'`, corpse tile, sentinel reset, stats-panel redraw - and that final redraw was missing from the cell entirely. Scope of the silence negative: a corpus census of every directly encoded call to every resident speaker primitive across the executable, every overlay and all four drivers, plus a raw port-access scan, with the death arm and the redraw additionally closed transitively; outside it are the display drivers' jump-table calling convention and music playback. |
| R380 | `issue #191` | `systems/combat.md` | 8.1, the closing paragraph of the banner subsection; 8, "The turn rule"; 6 and 6.1, the descriptor state list and the flags-byte table's `0x04` row; 8.2, the melee occupancy-lookup rejection list; 11, the C-Cast field-placement restatement of that same lookup | "The banner is unconditional once the actor passes the active-player gate, the Sword-of-Chaos gate and the **invisible-reveal / asleep early-outs**. Note that a free re-prompt after a refusal uses the short form and does **not** reprint the banner." Section 8's turn rule published the same model as "the parser keeps a **single** re-prompt flag". Section 6.1's flags-byte table published bit `0x04` as "Hidden / not-yet-revealed (invisible)", Section 6's descriptor summary listed "hidden/not-yet-revealed" among the per-turn states, Section 8.2's occupancy-lookup rejection list read "the occupant is dead-marked, **invisible**, or an empty/decoration slot", and Section 11 restated the same lookup as rejecting a slot "without hidden/not-yet-revealed bit `0x04`". | **Both halves are wrong.** (a) Only two things precede the banner - the active-player gate and the Sword-of-Chaos gate. The two status early-outs **follow** it, so a sleeping or dragged-under combatant gets its full banner and *then* `Zzzzz...` / `ARGH!` in place of a command. The `0x04` arm is the **dragged-under (Corpser-held)** state, not an "invisible-reveal"; invisibility is a different descriptor bit and is not tested on this path at all. (b) There are **two** re-prompt flags with two visibly different transcripts. Short, banner not reprinted: an unrecognised key, Ctrl-S, Ctrl-B, a refused step, a refused climb. **Full, the entire banner reprinted from its leading newline:** every shape-B letter, `D`, `W`, every `Can't!` refusal (Cast's and the six shape-A letters'), a failed `1`-`6` select, and a declined Escape - the larger of the two families. Neither spends the turn and neither runs the committed-action tail; that part of the old text stands. An engine built on the withdrawn sentence prints the banner once per turn and drops a line the original prints on most refusals. (c) **The bit name is withdrawn wherever it was published, not only in the Section 8.1 phrase.** Bit `0x04` is the **dragged-under (Corpser-held)** state; invisibility rides on bit `0x10`, the phase/blink bit. The flags-byte table row, the Section 6 state list, and the Section 11 restatement are corrected to say so, and the Section 8.2 rejection list loses its `invisible` entry outright: the melee occupancy lookup tests no invisibility flag at all, and what it rejects in that entry's place is the dragged-under bit together with the linked presentation record's hidden-frame marker. The table row is the load-bearing one - an engine built from it filters melee targets by invisibility, which is the behaviour this row withdraws, and lets a dragged-under combatant be targeted, which the original does not. |
| R381 | `issue #191` | `systems/combat.md`, `systems/inventory.md`, `systems/commands.md` | combat 8, the "Shape A" paragraph and the `C`/`G`/`J`/`O`/`P`/`R`/`S`/`U`/`Z` table rows that name its gate, and "The turn rule" raiser list; inventory 6 (combat `R`), 7 (combat `U`) and the U-Use implementer rule; commands 8.1 (combat `P`) | "**Shape A - the labelled prompt with a live-actor gate.** The helper prints the verb label, then requires that the acting combatant is still **alive**. A **dead** actor gets the short \"Can't!\" refusal", repeated as "Labelled prompt with the live-actor gate" in six rows and negated in two; and the `Z` row's "with no live-actor gate, **selecting the acting combatant's party slot instead of prompting**". The phrase "the live-actor gate" appeared verbatim in `systems/inventory.md` (combat `R`, and the U-Use implementer rule) and `systems/commands.md` (combat `P`), and `systems/inventory.md` section 7 spelled it out as "checks that the acting combatant is **still alive**"; combat 8's `C` row said the same thing as "unless **the caster is dead**, which re-prompts". | **The gate reads the party-side bit, not liveness**, and the dead-actor case it describes is unreachable - the round walker filters marked-dead slots before dispatch, so no dead actor ever reaches this handler. The refusal's one live population is a **monster acting under player control**, which is why `G`, `J`, `O`, `R`, `S` and `U` - and `C`-Cast, which carries its own copy of the same test - are the seven letters such an actor cannot use. The transcript is unchanged for party members, which is why the wrong test was invisible there; it inverts the outcome for a controlled monster, which the old text would have let use all six shape-A verbs freely. Separately, **`Z` prompts a monster-side actor**: inside an arena the shared status handler tests the acting slot's party side, opens a party actor's own sheet silently, and for a monster actor prints `Player: ` and runs the ordinary roster picker, because a monster descriptor has no roster slot to open. What that picker accepts is not published here. |
| R382 | `issue #191` | `systems/magic.md`, `catalogs/spell-list.md` | magic 8, "Summoning and conjuration"; spell-list, the Conjure/Swarm/Summon placement note in the implementer notes | That a creature these spells place prints the melee attack transcript unconditionally: magic 8 published its prompted turn as "the reduced turn banner ..., then `Attack-`, `Aim! `, `<target> missed!` on a failed roll, and `Nothing!` by any of three routes", and spell-list published "the reduced turn banner and the ordinary `Attack-` / `Aim! ` sequence". | **`Aim! ` and the melee cursor are scoped to a class whose reach selector is one.** The shared spell/weapon dispatcher prints `Aim! ` on its melee arm only; a class whose reach selector is above one takes the cast/effect arm instead and prints no `Aim! `, opens no cursor, and reaches neither the melee miss line nor any of the three `Nothing!` routes (`systems/combat.md` Sections 11 and 11.1, first published in the same pass). **Summon's Daemon is such a class**, so the withdrawn sentences are wrong for Summon's own creature - the spell whose row they annotate. Swarm's Insect Swarm is melee-reach and is unaffected; Conjure's four animal classes are not individually resolved, so both documents now direct an implementer to the creature's reach selector rather than to a fixed transcript. `Attack-` and the reduced banner are unaffected: they precede the dispatcher and print for every class. An engine built on the withdrawn text prints `Aim! ` and opens a targeting cursor for a summoned Daemon, where the original goes straight to the class effect arm. |

| R396 | `bada27e` | `systems/npc-schedules.md` | 4 (runtime table and initialization), 5, 6, 7, 11 | Runtime offset 0 has width "byte"; references to the "state byte"; offsets 2/4 are `target_x`/`target_y`, "currently-pursued X" and "Currently-pursued Y". | State is a full little-endian word, read and written at word width. X/Y are the NPC's current logical position, updated at every accepted queued step, not destination coordinates. The saved runtime layout is eight words including the previously omitted dialogue word at offset 10. |
| R397 | `bada27e` | `systems/npc-schedules.md` | 4 (shared per-tick bytes) | "Two per-tick scratch booleans ... an 'any NPC moved' flag and an 'any tile changed' flag"; town reads the first "to decide whether the screen needs a repaint". | The bytes hold an engagement kind and NPC roster index, cleared by the schedule pass and consumed by town event dispatch. They are saved at `0x1018` and `0x1019`; they are not redraw booleans. |
| R398 | `ec5f808` | `systems/shops.md` | 2 | Transport "is the only precondition between the Talk dispatch and the shop arm". | Both reached and current hour-selected waypoint must be 1 before the transport check. | R399 | `ec5f808` | `systems/npc-schedules.md`, `systems/town-mode.md` | npc-schedules 9; town-mode 14 | AI 4 is the "Approach-and-attack family"; AI 4/5 raise the "town-mode attack event"; AI 6 raises the "non-attack guard event". | AI 4/5 with nonzero dialogue request shared conversation dispatch, including shops and regime demands; ordinary contact raises no alarm. | R400 | `ec5f808` | `systems/npc-schedules.md` | 9, engagement paragraph | "there is always one full turn between" adjacency and its event; the shared gate says an NPC four tiles away "does nothing at all". | Adjacency is tested at engagement entry: a party move beside the NPC can trigger during that command's schedule pass; an NPC move into adjacency waits for a later invocation. AI 4 measures party-to-waypoint distance and wanders at cap 3 when that distance is at least four; AI 3/6 use current NPC distance. |
| R401 | `500abe7` | `systems/blackthorn.md`, `systems/conversation.md` | blackthorn 7a; conversation 2 step 5; issue #206 closing answer's exclusive-gate wording | A guard reaches the regime handler "only while" its reached waypoint carries approach behavior; "The shipped regime guards carry that behaviour on their middle waypoint only". | Behavior 4 rewrites to 1 and bypasses the guard gate; otherwise exact live guard sprite `0x70` with reached waypoint 1 and nonzero dialogue still dispatches, regardless of behavior. | R402 | `be2af40` | `systems/magic.md` | 5, step 8 | "Most handlers print a short success message: Light!, Wind change!, Protection!, View!, Resurrection!, Negate magic!, Summon Daemon!"; only "a handful print nothing"; "a success without a spell-specific message prints Success!". | Those named banners belong to scroll dispatch. In Lor and Vas Lor are silent on success at every hour; Section 5.1 gives all forty-eight spells' completion cases and helper-owned narration. |

Scroll prefix and rune label use the runic font; potion prefix uses runic font then its colour uses text. The Sceptre, Skull Keys and other named artifacts carry no decoration. Section 4.5 classifies all 38 U-Use names and the other inventory families. |

ENDMSG.DAT". | Only a first No asks the clarification. A first Yes goes directly to the saved-box check; effective Yes plus box enters victory. Every waiting outcome prints the program literal `"I see...` and one newline, pauses forty animation ticks, then prints record 10 whose leading newline supplies the blank row. |

| R407 | `f6c02e7` | `systems/audio.md` | 9; new 7.5 | "successful top-down walking" belongs to the explicit silence list. | Foot/horse town and overworld attempts play an initial two-part sting, normal horse commits can add a second, and accepted in-arena directional steps play the same sting. | R408 | `b5a3a87` | `systems/view.md` | 3 | Death vision "prints the selected member number" and does not "change party state"; cancelling returns "with nothing printed". | The failed Intelligence check prints Death vision! and applies one HP of damage with ordinary hit feedback, potentially killing that member; no number is printed. | R409 | `b5a3a87` | `systems/magic.md`, `catalogs/spell-list.md` | magic 4, 8, combat restore table; spell-list 5 | Awaken "wakes the first Sleeping party member found in roster order" and "has no selected-member prompt". | An Zu asks On who: and wakes only the selected member if Sleeping, with no generic success line. | R410 | `31e528c` | `systems/combat.md` | 7, 14 | If party actors remain and foes do not, the loop prints VICTORY! once, with no initial-foe gate specified. | The initial combat census arms the announcement only when a foe exists. | R411 | `31e528c` | `systems/karma.md`, `catalogs/gazetteer.md`, `systems/overworld.md`, `systems/magic.md` | karma 7; gazetteer 7; overworld moongate handoff; magic sources | Meditation begins by pressing M at a shrine coordinate; the coordinate table itself is the shrine-entry test; no party can stand on the ocean sentinel. | E enters a live mystic-shrine tile 0x19; M always mixes reagents. | R414 | `31e528c` | `systems/commands.md` | 5.2, 5.3 | Search... is the in-place form and its ellipsis denotes a selection on another surface. | It is the dungeon echo and precedes the shared Dir- relative chooser; Ahead/Left/Right/Here and Space/Pass choose or cancel the target. |
| R415 | `16de078` | `systems/inventory.md`, `catalogs/item-list.md` | inventory 7/7.1; item-list 8 | Skull Keys attempt the lock helper only in supported non-combat scenes; the per-family gate refuses combat. | They spend a key and run the cardinal-prompted unlock helper in combat as well as town/overworld. | R447 | `17ad9af` | `systems/magic.md` | 8, direct damage | Negative Missile/Fireball damage uses the normal miss/no-damage path. | Nonpositive damage uses the target-named grazed result and suppresses later wound or kill lines, matching combat Section 11.1. |

| R450 | `7928aec` | `systems/input.md` | 3, no-key path | With no key, the logical cursor X-position is rolled back by one cell; the next iteration depends on this erase-or-rewind step. | The no-key path requests the calibrated one-tick delay. | R459 | `610d3f0` | `systems/inventory.md` | 4, 4.3 | The initial Z selector's explicit none/retry result only redraws the prompt; zero is accepted only in the active-player prompt and ignored elsewhere. | The initial Z selector accepts zero and opens shared Equipment without selecting a member. | R462 | `18ac92d` | `systems/audio.md` | 6.1 | Magic absorbed! is printed in one scene and in a second scene when a state flag is set. | The text is Absorbed! with a trailing newline. | R463 | `0d69abe` | `systems/town-mode.md` | 10 | Look does not consume a turn; Look and inspect-style actions are exempt from the epilogue; Look and cancelled prompts do not run underfoot effects. | Look is acted, including direction cancellation, and reaches the ordinary town epilogue. No blanket inspection/cancellation exemption exists; other cancellations retain their command-specific cost. The existing effect gates still govern walkers. |
| R464 | `0d69abe` | `systems/time.md` | 5, 10 | Look is a no-turn example; talking to an NPC, opening a door and looking at a tile take no time and invoke no cleanup. | Modal input waiting is distinct from command completion. Look/Open/Ready/Z keep acted; Look follows the ordinary town/overworld epilogue and dungeon input-time/post-action rules. Other commands follow their published result contract. |
| R465 | `0d69abe` | `systems/dungeon-mode.md` | 12 | L-Look does not advance time; it is a free action. | Look retains acted even on focus cancellation and selects the normal dungeon post-action pass. The dungeon loop advances time at its input boundary, with no free-Look exception. |
| R466 | `0d69abe` | `systems/overworld.md` | 5 | Looking at the sky, opening inventory and talking to no one cost zero time; only a successful action advances the clock. | The epilogue is selected by command status, not success or movement. Look/Open/Ready/Z retain acted, and Look cancellation reaches the ordinary two-minute epilogue. Unknown commands still return no action. |
| R467 | `5d906f7`; issue #259 first answer | `systems/inventory.md` | 4.4, 7.1 | The message window is untouched by the picker; each family begins at column six on the same row as Item. | The message descriptor is retained, but an EGA panel overflow shifts existing message pixels. Completion uses the retained cursor and can appear below the displaced prompt; no fixed gap or indent literal exists. |
| R468 | `5d906f7` | `systems/text-output.md` | 1, 5, 10.5, 11 | Scrolling stays within the active window and never touches another; every overflow scrolls that rectangle one row without blanking, and the triggering or next output immediately covers the exposed band. | Cursor updates stay with the active descriptor. EGA requests with left edge 192 shift a fixed right-side strip, including panel callers; other left edges use general scrolling. The fixed strip is unblanked and subsequent message-band repaint is not guaranteed. |
| R469 | `5d906f7` | `systems/display-driver-abi.md` | 9.5 | Every text scroll uses the fixed one-cell path regardless of distance; the caller immediately paints over the unblanked bottom band. | The requested left edge alone selects the fixed path, so upper-panel calls can move message pixels. Other left edges use the general path. A later repaint of the exposed band is not guaranteed. |
| R470 | issue #262 | `systems/doors-and-z-transitions.md` | 9, Overworld K | After the shared pre-action gate, outdoor K-Klimb probes the target tile in the current facing direction. | There is no facing probe and no separate pre-action gate: that gate is the shared direction prompt. Outdoor K echoes the chosen direction's name on the verb row, and the pass key cancels the command outright; every other key including Escape is re-read. The gear refusal ends the command before any direction is requested. The step-commit routine, previously called the resident climb/move helper, is an overlay routine reached through a resident thunk and emits no text. |
| R471 | issue #262 | `systems/dungeon-mode.md`, `systems/combat.md` | dungeon-mode 13.1, 14.1; combat 4 | A two-way dungeon ladder is recognised by the resident tile-restoration flag in room-entry mode, that flag is the Klimb handler's second reader, and the combat framer's clear of it must not be reordered ahead of a Klimb. | The Klimb dispatcher was read end to end and consults no room-entry mode and no tile-restoration flag. Two-way is the two-way cell class alone; the only flag the dispatcher reads is the climbing-gear byte, which selects the up-only arm rather than raising a prompt. There is no Klimb ordering constraint on the framer's clear. The flag's operand set and setter census stand; the census's second read is now unattributed. |
| R472 | issue #262 | `systems/dungeon-mode.md` | 13.1, dungeon level-change spells | The Up and Down spells test the destination cell and refuse it when it is in the base 0x0 class or in the wall and door-presentation families 0xB? through 0xE?. | Exactly inverted. The test accepts only the open-passage (base) class and refuses every other class, ladders, chests, fountains, pits, fields, room states, walls and door variants included. The low half of the cell byte is ignored, so every variant within the accepted class passes. The later comparisons against wall and door-presentation classes are unreachable. The climb word prints before both the edge check and the destination test, and the edge check precedes the destination test, so a stack-edge exit is unaffected by the destination cell. |
| R473 | issue #262 | `systems/commands.md`, `systems/doors-and-z-transitions.md` | commands 7; doors 2, 4 | Dungeon Open checks the underfoot dungeon class: door cells route to the dungeon door opener, chest cells open, and unrelated cells refuse. Dungeon Open matches purely on the high nibble. | There is no door arm. The dungeon Open handler recognises exactly two classes, the closed chest and the open chest, and refuses every other cell; the call previously read as a door opener is the shared trap resolver. It also reads the cell's low three bits as the trap sub-type, so it does not match on the high nibble alone, and it awaits no direction. |
| R474 | issue #262 | `catalogs/gazetteer.md` | 9, runtime error handling | If a dungeon return coordinate is missing, keep the party in dungeon mode or fail the load with a diagnostic rather than dropping to a default surface cell. | No per-save return-coordinate datum exists. Return positions are never stored: they are read at the moment of leaving from the two static per-scene coordinate tables of Section 5.1, by every exit route, and entering a location caches nothing. Every legal scene byte has a populated row, so the branch is unreachable and any player-visible message on it has no original counterpart. |
| R475 | issue #262 | `systems/magic.md`, `catalogs/spell-list.md` | magic 8 (Open bullet and the directed-helper preamble), 8 handler-family map; spell-list row 6 | The dungeon arm of Open acts on the party's dungeon cell when that cell is a door/urn-class cell, prints a disarm line when the cell carries the trapped variant bit, and shares the family's shape of prompting for a direction. | The class is the closed-chest class. The disarm line is gated on the cell's lowest bit, while the variant/visit bit is the one the rewrite preserves rather than the one it tests. The dungeon arm prompts for nothing: it takes the cell underfoot, else the cell one step along the party's current facing, wrapped on both axes. It prints Disarmed! then Chest opened! and returns already-narrated; it never reaches the trap resolver. Its scene gate is wider than the dungeon arms of O-Open and G-Get. |
| R476 | issue #262 | `systems/commands.md`, `systems/containers.md`, `catalogs/item-list.md` | commands 7; containers 3; item-list Sandalwood Box row and Section 9 | The G-Get accepted set is "special pickup-category markers and the loose-object visual family", a visual filter distinct from the object's separate inventory-add code; the byte that makes Get legal is "not necessarily the same byte that selects the item awarded". | There is one byte. The record's inventory class byte is both the acceptance test and the grant dispatcher's selector: accepted when it is below sixteen, or is the Moonstone class, the magic carpet class, or one of the four regalia/shard classes. The record's auxiliary byte carries the quantity or sub-kind. The `CASTLE:0` Sandalwood Box slot is accepted *because* its class is below sixteen. |
| R477 | issue #262 | `systems/commands.md`, `systems/containers.md` | commands 7; containers 1, 2, 4 | A matching hidden object found by surface/town Search prints the found result and dispatches the object through the same inventory-add path used by pickup/container commands. | Surface Search's object branch accepts **only** the container class and hands it to the trap narrator, which contains no inventory grant at all. Search grants nothing from the object table; its Moonstone and fixed-treasure results stage an object for a following Get, and only the rare-reagent harvest credits a counter directly. |
| R478 | issue #262 | `systems/commands.md`, `systems/containers.md`, `systems/karma.md` | commands 7; containers 7; karma 8 negative-boundaries table | Get's tile fallbacks include "borrowing a table item"; the borrow branch is a furniture/table cell ("borrowed-furniture") that mutates the tile and plays feedback. | The two tiles that answer `Borrowed!` are the wall-torch pair `0xB0`/`0xB1`; the laden tables `0x9A..0x9C` are the separate eating case, which prints `Mmmmm...!` and does debit moral standing. The borrow branch also overwrites the party-dirty byte outright and calls a further shared resident routine in non-combat scenes, so it is not a bare tile swap. Its published torch-counter effect and its no-standing-debit boundary are unchanged, though the identification of the byte it sets to one hundred is now recorded as open in `OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`. |
| R479 | issue #262 | `systems/conversation.md`, `systems/shops.md`, `systems/blackthorn.md`, `systems/npc-schedules.md` | conversation 2, step 5; shops 2; blackthorn 7a; npc-schedules 9.2 | A guard-sprite NPC dispatches normally only when **both** its current waypoint is waypoint 1 and its dialog index is non-zero; the shop trigger is not waypoint-conditioned beyond the open-for-business check. | The instruction is a **parity** test on the current waypoint index, not equality with one: an even index always gives the guard refusal, an odd index with a non-zero dialogue index always dispatches. The same parity bit gates the shop path, so an even-index shopkeeper prints the merchant brush-off without the open-for-business check being consulted. The two readings agree at the indices `0`, `1` and `2` that shipped schedules use and diverge above them. |
| R480 | issue #263 | `systems/combat.md` | 9, movement fallback and teleport | Ordinary AI stepping "first tries the target vector on one axis, with randomized axis priority, then falls back to random cardinal tries when the direct axes are blocked". | The two axes are not symmetric. One per-turn draw over the inclusive range `0..255` selects the branch: above the midpoint the actor offers the X-displaced candidate and falls back to the Y-displaced one if that is refused; at or below the midpoint it offers **only** the Y-displaced candidate and never tries X. An implementation that offers both axes in a random order matches the original on the high branch and diverges on the low one. A candidate whose displacement on the offered axis is zero is the actor's own cell and is always refused. The random-cardinal fallback after the axis attempts is unchanged (R311). See Section 9.1. |
| R481 | issue #264 | `systems/inventory.md` | 4.5 | Three sentences of the picker row geometry: an uncounted `Shard/Falsehd` or `Shard/Cowrdce` is "twelve text cells followed by one padding space" and `Shard/Hatred` "eleven cells followed by two spaces"; a counted row's "name has ten cells in columns 4 through 13"; and "a quantity-one carpet starts with one space, `1`, one space, and `Magic Crpt`, then one padding space". | All three are wrong by one cell or by shape. The shard labels are thirteen, twelve and thirteen cells: `Shard/Falsehd` and `Shard/Cowrdce` fill the thirteen-cell interior with **no** padding, `Shard/Hatred` takes one padding space, and the quantity-one `Magic Crpt` row is thirteen cells with no padding at all. There is no ten-cell name field: the name is as long as the label, and a label that will not fit the rest of the display line is moved whole to the next one by the shared word-wrapping printer, so a counted shard row occupies **two** display lines and is neither clipped nor emitted as one over-long line. The quantity field is never clamped - two cells for 1..99, three for 100..254 - and a three-cell quantity shifts the selector and name one column right. |
| R482 | issue #263 | `systems/combat.md` | 9, movement fallback and teleport | When all four random-cardinal attempts fail, "the routine still reports the action as consumed unless the final draw happened to be the first direction tried", with zero committed displacement. That clause was first published in R311's corrected column and carried into Section 9. | The discriminator is the **direction code the last draw produced**, not its relationship to the first attempt. A last draw of any cardinal other than the first of the four direction codes commits a zero-length step, reports the actor as moved, and runs the post-step in-arena test on the unchanged position; a last draw of the first code reports that the actor did not move. With every draw pinned to one direction - so that the final draw is always the first one tried - the two outcomes still diverge, which the withdrawn wording cannot express. The rest of R311 is unchanged: the fallback is still four independent draws with no neighbour scan. |
| R483 | issue #263 | `systems/combat.md` | 9, morale writer | Between one-quarter and one-half of class maximum HP the wound-score classifier "sets fleeing on 252 of 256 possible random-byte results". | Inverted. A roll of 251 or less commits with the fleeing bit cleared; only a roll of 252 or more (4 of 256) sets it. A heavily wounded monster therefore rarely flees on morale alone; the certain flee is below one quarter. Re-derived from the shipped combat overlay on 2026-09-12. |
| R484 | issue #259 | `systems/text-output.md`, `systems/display-driver.md` | text-output 10.1, "Row 24"; display-driver 7, bottom-gutter paragraph and zone table | Absolute text row 24 "is cleared to black when the frame is painted and stays black for the rest of the session"; it "is left black by the phase-0 clear and is never painted again by any gameplay path"; the bottom gutter is "always black". | No gameplay path emits **text** into row 24, which is the surviving half. The row does not stay black: every message-strip scroll - the left-edge-192 fast path, which ordinary message overflow and upper-panel overflow both take - writes row 24's columns 24..39 with unblanked bytes fetched from beyond the visible raster, and does not clear them. Those bytes are what the message window's vacated bottom row inherits on the next scroll, so a session's first scroll shows a correctly blank bottom row and later ones do not. Columns 0..23 of the row are still never written after the frame paint. |
| R485 | issue #259 | `systems/text-output.md`, `systems/stats-panel.md` | text-output 10.1, "Column 39"; stats-panel 3, panel field width | "The stats window spans columns 24..39, but the panel only ever writes columns 24..38, because the roster and counter boxes are fifteen cells wide." | True of the resting panel content - the roster rows, the counters row and the date row - and withdrawn as a statement about the window. The item picker borrows the same window, and a list row whose label ends on the window's last writable cell writes screen column 39, one cell outside the drawn frame and over its right vertical rule. `systems/inventory.md` Section 4.5 has published the two-cell-past-the-rule case since R481; this row reconciles the sibling. `systems/stats-panel.md` Section 3 carried the same sentence and is narrowed with it to the resting roster, counters and date content. An implementation that clips window-1 output at column 38 loses that cell. |
| R486 | issue #266 | `systems/conversation.md`, `systems/quest-flags.md`, `formats/tlk.md`, `systems/karma.md` | conversation 7 (`0x88` row), 7.6; quest-flags 3 step 4; tlk 9 (`0x88` row); karma 4.1 routing block | The ASK-WHO name match is "a match against a **live** party member", and the setter fires "after the player types a line that names a live party member". | The scan never reads member status. It walks roster slots below the current party-count byte and compares only the name field, so a dead, poisoned, sleeping or charmed member matches exactly as a healthy one does and sets the bit exactly as a healthy one does. The correct qualifier is "a roster slot within the current party count". An implementation that filters the scan by status will silently fail the introduce-yourself branch, and its one-time reward, for any party carrying a casualty. Re-derived by executing the original handler across the status values on 2026-09-12. |
| R487 | issue #266 | `systems/conversation.md` | 7 (`0x88` row) | The ASK-WHO prompt "is the engine's own **four-row** literal", and "the stream around it carries only the closing and opening quotes". | Both are withdrawn. (a) The prompt is not four rows: at the message window's width the quoted question wraps, so the sequence renders as the opening quote and the first words of the question, the rest of the question with its closing quote, a blank row, `You respond-`, and the colon row. (b) The quotes are not the stream's. The handler emits the opening quote before the question itself, and each acknowledgement carries its own opening quote; the stream's quote immediately after the control byte - present at 37 of the 48 shipped occurrences - is discarded by the runner's duplicate-quote rule, and a closing quote directly precedes the control byte at only 11 of the 48. An implementation that takes its quotes from the stream prints none in front of the question or the acknowledgement and prints a stray lone-quote row after it. Section 7.6 "What ASK-WHO prints" now carries the full sequence. |
| R488 | issue #267 | `systems/main-loop.md`, `systems/input.md` | main-loop 9; input 12, the "can take long enough to be perceptible" bullet and the "first-tick after mode entry" bullet | "On the very first tick after a mode entry, an additional full-panel repaint runs, painting the side panels, status bars, and frame borders that the in-loop renderer otherwise leaves alone"; "a first-frame panel repaint" listed among what the idle world tick can take long enough to do; and "the world tick uses this [one-shot first-tick hint] to do a fuller initial re-paint". | No world-tick path repaints the stats panel, on a first tick after mode entry or on any other, and neither does the per-frame active-object animator. The tick's tail calls the **ambient-audio tick** when the first-tick hint is non-zero - which is almost every tick - and normalises the hint afterwards. Everything that paints the panel is either a routine that has just changed a displayed number or a mode loop draining a refresh request at its command prompt: `systems/stats-panel.md` Sections 2.2 to 2.4. An engine that repaints the panel from the tick, or from live state every frame, shows deferred changes one prompt early; that is the premise behind all three cases reported in issue #267. |
| R489 | issue #267 | `systems/combat.md` | 7, "The middle tier's flag" | The panel refresh request has "a single consumer shape: each of the four world/combat mode loops - combat, dungeon, outdoor and town - reads it once at the top of its per-turn entry point and, if it is set, redraws the full party stats panel and clears it." | The four consumers exist and all four test-repaint-clear, but two of them are narrower than "once at the top of its per-turn entry point", and an implementation built on that wording drains at points the original does not. **Combat** drains at the top of a human-controlled actor's command prompt, on that actor's *first* keystroke only: a refused or multi-part command re-enters the prompt below the test and leaves the request pending, so combat drains per acting character, not per round or per turn. **Town** drains only on its input helper's full-prompt arm; its quick-poll arm does not test the request at all. The overworld and town consumers sit in the shared input helper after its world-tick call and before the prompt newline and key read; the dungeon's is the first thing its render-and-poll step does. `systems/stats-panel.md` Section 2.3. |
| R490 | issue #268 | `systems/blackthorn.md` | 6 (known script beats), 6.3 (vector table row) | The 59-byte audience cutscene vector is the "per-question intermission", listed among the script beats and tabulated under that name, i.e. a beat that plays between the interrogation's questions. | It never plays between questions. The vector has exactly one reference in the whole overlay, from a helper with exactly one caller, and that caller is the interrogation's terminating join. It plays **once**, on exit, on each of the three branches that end the audience well: the two solo records and the correct-answer merciful-death execution. It is now the audience exit beat of Section 4.2. The vector's own observable sequence — the guard's westward step, the cobble buffered at `(0,4)` and exposed on the explicit tick, the door replacing it, the tableau and guards clearing, the final six-repetition hold — is accurate and unchanged; only the name and the placement are withdrawn. An implementation that plays it after each of up to four questions plays a roughly five-second animation up to four times too often and omits it from the branch that actually has it. |
| R491 | issue #268 | `systems/timing.md` | 4 (hardware-tick wait), 5.2 | A one-tick BIOS timer request is skipped when the boot calibration value reports a CPU at or below the original IBM PC baseline, and "any faster host performs the real one-tick wait". | The skip half stands; the "any faster host" half does not. The threshold test is a **signed** comparison of the whole calibration word, so a word with its high bit set compares as below the baseline and takes the skip exactly as a small value does. An implementation that models the calibration word as an unsigned count agrees with the original across the low half of the range and diverges across the entire high half, silently turning every one-tick presentation pause back on. Executed across the threshold on 2026-09-12. Which side of the threshold a real install lands on during gameplay is separately open (`OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`). |
| R492 | issue #271 | `systems/karma.md` | 7, shrine-meditation opening paragraph | "the meditation handler renders the kneeling avatar", with the entry table giving no interval between the Approach and Kneel records. | Both the stage and the pose are wrong. The animation is an **approach walk** rendered by the shrine *presentation*, before the meditation handler is entered at all, using a walking pose that is a different tile from the kneeling one and that never animates. It is nine frames - four of them with no avatar drawn anywhere - stepping up the display grid's centre column from the bottom row to one row short of the altar, at one world step plus a two-part sting plus four world steps per frame. The meditation handler's own contribution is to replace the walking pose with the kneeling pose and repaint once, which is the forty-sixth world step of the interval, and only then print the Kneel record. The kneeling pose, unlike the walking one, is an animated four-frame family while it is on screen. An implementation built on the withdrawn sentence renders a kneel where the original renders a walk, renders it in the wrong handler, and prints the Approach and Kneel records with no interval between them where the original spends forty-six world steps, forty-five one-tick delay requests and nine stings. Section 7 now carries the interval column and the pacing contract. Executed in two independent harnesses on 2026-09-12. |
| R493 | issue #269 | `systems/blackthorn.md` | 7, rescue contract step 6 | The rescue's party restoration, in full: "Restore every party member: the member's status is reset to able-bodied and their current hit points are set to their maximum." | **Both halves are withdrawn.** The restore is neither unconditional nor lossless. The cinematic dispatches the shared revive routine per in-party slot, and that routine acts **only** on a slot whose stored status is Dead: it sets the status to able-bodied, sets current hit points to one, restores magic points from the class letter (Avatar and Mage the full Intelligence value, Bard half of it, every other class untouched), and then - **when the moral standing is below ninety-eight** - rewrites experience to experience times standing divided by one hundred, recomputes the level from the resulting experience, and recomputes maximum hit points as thirty times that level. Only then does the cinematic copy that newly recomputed maximum into current hit points. A party wipe therefore **costs experience in proportion to the party's karma**, and members can return at a lower level with a lower maximum than they had before dying; an engine built on the old sentence silently preserves progression the original destroys. A slot that is **not** Dead is left entirely alone by the revive routine and prints `Not dead!` instead, though the cinematic's own current-equals-maximum copy still heals it to full. `systems/blackthorn.md` Section 7, "Party restoration". |
| R494 | issue #269 | `systems/audio.md` | cue producer census, "Blackthorn rescue envelopes" row; 8.6.2 | The rescue's envelope content: "One fixed six-row sequence after the refuge tableau first redraws the party actor." | The cinematic has **two** envelope sites, not one. Besides the six-row sequence, its party-restoration step plays one further envelope **per in-party slot**, in ascending slot order, immediately before that slot's restore dispatch, at phase increment `36400 / (slot + 7)` - 5200, 4550, 4044, 3640, 3309 and 3033 for slots zero to five - with idle count 1, 30,000 iterations, initial comparison 2000 and comparison delta +2. An empty roster produces no notes; the note count always equals the party count. Because the census row named the six-row sequence as the whole of the cinematic's envelope content, this contradicts it rather than merely extending it. The +2 delta is not new with this loop: the monster-possession/faint recipe of Section 8.3 is its near twin. `systems/audio.md` Section 8.6.2, `systems/blackthorn.md` Section 7. |
| R495 | issue #269 | `formats/karma-dat.md` | 2 (File Structure) | How the file is read: "To read record `n`, a reader starts at the beginning of the file and skips `n` NUL-terminated records." | The original does not walk the file. The consumer holds each band's start position itself, seeks straight to the selected one, and issues **one fixed-length request** - two thousand bytes, from a 761-byte file - into a scratch buffer, so the read always runs to end of file, always returns fewer bytes than requested, and relies on the record's own NUL terminator rather than on the length read. A reader that validates the requested length, or that treats a short read as an error, diverges on every verdict it prints. Skipping terminators happens to yield the same five bodies for the five defined bands, which is why the difference was invisible; it does not yield the same behaviour for a short read, a truncated asset, or an out-of-range band. `formats/karma-dat.md` Section 2. |
| R496 | issue #270 | `systems/text-output.md` | 10.2 (the command-echo cycle) | The prompt pair's gating: "The town and dungeon loops emit the pair unconditionally on every polled turn." | **The dungeon half stands** and is now executed - that helper has no gate at all between entry and the feed except the pending stats repaint. **The town half is withdrawn.** Town's helper emits the pair on its full-prompt arm only; the loop arms that request immediately before the call and clears it immediately after, and the town-only re-poll result - produced by keying digits at the harpsichord - returns to the call site with the request already cleared. That path is a poll that emits **neither** feed nor marker, and it is the only ordinary command path in the mode that emits neither, so an engine that opens a prompt row for every harpsichord digit diverges visibly. Separately, the pair is emitted from seven sites rather than one: each mode's input helper, plus a second site inside the town loop and the overworld loop on the arm where nobody in the party can act, which emits the pair itself, prints the sleep line and skips the poll. `systems/text-output.md` Section 10.2, `systems/main-loop.md` Section 6. |
| R497 | issue #270 | `systems/text-output.md` | 10.2 (the command-echo cycle) | The overworld prompt gate: the one-byte flag "is cleared only on the 'already sailing that way' path, where the loop synthesises a repeat movement command rather than reading a key." | The condition is wider: the flag is cleared by **any direction taken while the party is aboard a ship**, including a pressed direction that turns the ship to a new heading. The comparison of the requested direction against the ship's stored heading decides only whether the heading is rewritten; both arms fall through onto the same clear. On foot the flag is never touched, and the helper re-arms it immediately after testing it, so exactly one prompt pair is suppressed. The published **purpose** - keeping synthesised sailing turns from accumulating empty prompt rows - is confirmed. An engine that suppresses only on the repeat-heading path emits a stray prompt row on every course change under sail. `systems/text-output.md` Section 10.2, `systems/overworld.md` Section 5. |
| R498 | issue #270 | `systems/commands.md` | 5.1 (the line prompt is a glyph, not a character) | The same unconditional reading, twice: "Every mode's turn loop opens its input line with the same two steps: emit a newline into the message window, then draw that one triangle", and "It is emitted exactly once per input line." | Both sentences need the qualifications of R496 and R497, which this section restated without them. The pair comes from seven sites rather than one; the overworld suppresses it for one prompt after any direction taken aboard a ship; and the town re-poll after a harpsichord digit emits neither feed nor marker. What survives is the narrower claim that **where** the pair is emitted it is emitted exactly once, never twice for one line. Correcting `systems/text-output.md` Section 10.2 alone would have left this section stale, and this is the section a command implementer reads. `systems/commands.md` Section 5.1. |
| R499 | issue #269 | `systems/magic.md` | 8 (spell effects), "The resurrection experience adjustment" | The direction of the resurrection experience rescale: "When that selector is below 98, the helper rescales the target's experience by multiplying by 100 and dividing by the selector before recomputing level." | **The two operands are the other way round.** The helper multiplies the target's experience by the moral-standing selector and divides by one hundred, so the rescale is a **penalty scaled by karma** and never a bonus. The published direction reverses the sign of the effect - it rewards a low standing with more experience than the member died with - and at a standing of zero it divides by zero, which the original never does (it scales to zero experience and returns the member at level one). Executed against the shipped overlay: for a member holding 800 experience the helper yields 0, 200, 400 and 776 at standings 0, 25, 50 and 97, each of which recomputes through the same published halving ladder to levels 1, 3, 4 and 4 and maxima of 30, 90, 120 and 120. The 98-and-above arm is unchanged, except that level and maximum hit points are still recomputed from the untouched experience. This is the same helper the total-party-defeat rescue dispatches into, so the two documents had opposite signs for one routine; `systems/blackthorn.md` Section 7 "Party restoration" and R493 carry the rescue-side contract. |
| R500 | issue #269 | `systems/magic.md` | 8 (spell effects), the resurrection helper | The class arm of the mana rebuild: "Avatar, Mage, and the default class branch receive mana equal to Intelligence; Bard receives half Intelligence." | There is no default branch. The helper tests the class letter three ways and writes magic points only for Avatar, Mage and Bard - Avatar and Mage the full Intelligence value, Bard half of it; every other class letter falls past the write entirely and keeps whatever magic points the record already held. An implementation built on the withdrawn sentence hands a Fighter the Intelligence value as mana on every resurrection. The rule is the same three-way one already published for the completed long-camp recovery block (`systems/magic.md` Section 11, `systems/rest-and-camp.md` Section 5), which is why the discrepancy is a transcription error rather than a disputed reading. |
| R501 | issue #269/#270 audit | `systems/karma.md` | 8 (Codex urn reading), the tile `0x41` paragraph | The E-Enter refusal on tile `0x41` spelled as `Enter What?` with a capital `W`. | The stored literal is `Enter what?` with a lower-case `w`, as `systems/commands.md` Sections 3 and 5.2 and `systems/town-mode.md` Section 10 already spell it. A one-character transcription slip, but a printed literal an implementation can be byte-checked against, and the only spelling of it anywhere in the repository that disagreed with the other three. No other claim in that paragraph changes. |
| R502 | issue #270 | `systems/stats-panel.md` | 2.1 (complete side-effect census), 3 (panel geometry), 6 (the counters row) | That the full-panel refresh "also **requests no scroll** and issues no display command against the message window's rectangle", offered as the reason a caller may repaint the panel in the middle of an unfinished message-window frame without disturbing it. | The refresh has one arm that **does** request a scroll. The counters row's ship variant places a fixed label mid-row and follows it with the hull number at its natural width, so a hull of 100 or more writes the stats window's last writable column and carries that window's cursor onto the panel's bottom row; the row sequence's next line feed is then emitted from that bottom row, overruns it, and requests a scroll under the **stats window's own** rectangle. The driver selects its fixed right-strip body by the requested left edge alone and both right-hand windows share that edge, so the request lifts the already-painted message history by one cell row while the message cursor does not move - a visible gap an engine that models the refresh as scroll-free will never open. Conditions: a repaint actually running, a non-combat scene, a ship-family transport marker, and a hull of 100 or more. The withdrawn sentence remains true for every hull below 100, for every non-ship marker and in combat scenes, which is every state the original claim's single executed repaint covered. Reproduced standalone and inside both plane-changing exits. Whether a party-held hull reaches 100 in stock play is separately open (`OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`); the delivered Frigate starts at 99 (`systems/vehicles.md`). `systems/stats-panel.md` Sections 2.1, 3 and 6, and the dependent sentence in `systems/blackthorn.md` Section 7.2, which is scoped with it. |
| R503 | issue #270 | `systems/text-output.md` | 10.1 (the three windows the gameplay screen installs), "Column 39" | That the stats window's *resting* content - the six roster rows, the counters row and the date row - "writes **only** columns 24..38", the survivor of the earlier wider claim R485 withdrew. | The counters row's ship variant breaks it: at a hull condition of 100 or more the third digit lands in column **39** and the cursor wraps onto the panel's bottom row. That is resting content, not the item picker R485 was about, so the narrowed negative is now false in its own right. The consequence is not only the lost cell: the wrap is what lets the panel's next line feed overrun and request the strip scroll of R502, so an implementation that clips window-1 output at column 38 loses the cell **and** never reproduces the displacement. `systems/text-output.md` Section 10.1, `systems/stats-panel.md` Sections 2.1 and 6. |
| R504 | issue #270 | `systems/dungeon-mode.md` | 13.2 (leaving the dungeon), the shared-narration bullet | The rendering of the dungeon exit line: "a line-feed-led `Exit to ` followed **on the same row** by the plane name, then a blank row". | Both halves are wrong. The plane name does **not** share the row: `Exit to ` ends the row's free space, the printer breaks there, and the break costs a real emitted line feed, so the name occupies its own row. And the stored line ends in **two** feeds, not one, so **two** blank rows separate the plane name from the next command prompt's marker row. An engine built on the withdrawn sentence renders `Exit to Britannia!` on one row and spends one advance too few, leaving its whole message history one row lower than the original's from that transition onward - which is exactly the reported symptom. `systems/doors-and-z-transitions.md` Section 12.1 has always rendered these rows correctly and was not affected; it now also carries the advance accounting, which is what the dungeon chapter's wording is brought into line with. `systems/dungeon-mode.md` Section 13.2. |
| R505 | issue #273 audit | `catalogs/tile-catalog.md` | 10 (vehicle tiles), the mounting sentence | "Mounting a vehicle moves the avatar's sprite from the party-sprite run (`320..335`) to the vehicle run." | Both halves are wrong. The party's sprite does not live in `320..335` at any time outside a combat arena, and mounting does not draw the party as the vehicle object. On foot the party draws tile `284`; mounted on a horse it draws `274` facing east and `275` facing west; on a magic carpet `276` and `277`; in a skiff one of `296..299`; aboard a frigate one of `288..295`. The riderless horse pair `272..273` and the dropped carpet `283` are the world **objects** boarding consumes and exiting leaves behind, never what the party is drawn as, and `272..287` is one party-and-vehicle band holding objects, mounted frames and the on-foot sprite together. An engine built on the withdrawn sentence draws a riderless horse where the original draws a rider, and looks for the party's sprite in a run of person art it never enters. `catalogs/tile-catalog.md` Sections 3, 3.1 and 10; the mapping is corroborated in `systems/vehicles.md` Section 2, which was already correct in transport-marker terms. |
| R506 | issue #273 audit | `catalogs/tile-catalog.md` | 3.1, the shipped-art row for `332..335` | That `332..335` is "the Avatar class sprite, **also used by every class outside the three above**". | An unrecognised class letter is given **no** sprite, not the avatar's. The arena seating pass maps nine class letters onto four families - Avatar to `332..335`, Bard/Shepherd/Tinker to `324..327`, Druid/Mage to `320..323`, Fighter/Paladin/Ranger to `328..331` - and every other letter falls past the write entirely, leaving both of that record's tile bytes at zero so nothing is drawn for the member. `systems/combat.md` Section 5 has always published this correctly, including the slot-allocation consequence that a zero tile byte makes the record read as free; it is the catalogue row that was wrong. |
| R507 | issue #273 audit | `systems/overworld.md`, `catalogs/tile-catalog.md`, `systems/vehicles.md` | overworld 9.2 (transit stage A); tile-catalog 6 (moongate cells) and 11 (effect tiles); vehicles 2 (the transient-override paragraph) | That the moon-gate swallow "switches the party sprite to tile `0x116`"; in the catalogue, that tile `0x116`'s own artwork "serves as the party-vanishing sprite during a gate transit"; and, in the vehicle document, that the transient written during the transit is "a moongate-transit frame". | These read a **marker byte** as an **atlas index**. The swallow writes actor byte `0x16`, which the rasteriser tests for immediately before forming an index and paints as nothing; because it paints nothing it never reaches tile `0x116` at all. Tile `0x116` is a different thing that happens to appear in the same presentation - the scratch tile the gate composition builds its frames in - which is exactly why the two must not be conflated. `systems/blackthorn.md` Section 6.1 has always carried the correct rule for the same byte ("Draw nothing. In actor storage this must not be interpreted as full atlas tile `0x116`"), so the repository disagreed with itself. Two suppressions also differ and an engine needs both: actor byte `0x16` leaves the player's slot live and merely unpainted, leaving the cell's existing pixels intact, while a marker of zero empties the slot so the walk skips it and terrain shows through. |
| R508 | issue #273 audit | `systems/vehicles.md` | 2 (vehicle state), the `0x1C` row | That marker value `0x1D` "is the second frame of the on-foot sprite pair". | The shipped art does not support it: tile `285` differs from the on-foot sprite `284` in more than one hundred and fifty of 256 pixels and their non-black palettes are disjoint, `285` being drawn entirely in blues and cyans. An engine must not animate the party on foot between the two tiles. The rest of the row stands unchanged - `0x1D` is accepted by both "party is on foot" predicates and no shipped writer produces it - but what the tile depicts, and why the predicates admit the value, are now open rather than answered (`OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`). |
| R509 | issue #273 audit | `systems/karma.md` | 7 (shrine meditation), the two-poses paragraph | That because the renderer copies the stored party sprite into the party's own slot every tick, "`0x11C` is also what the overworld paints for a party walking on foot", stated without qualification. | The copy into the slot is unqualified; what the *screen* gets is not. The compositor is terrain-aware for the on-foot value, so on a chair, a bed or a floor link the party's cell is painted as the occupied-fixture tile instead, and on dense forest (`0x0A`) and the standard of Britannia (`0xEC`) the party's stamp is suppressed entirely - a party walking on foot through dense forest is not drawn at all. Dense forest is ordinary, walkable overworld terrain, so this is not a corner case: an engine that paints the stored sprite unconditionally shows an avatar standing in forest the original hides. The identity claim the sentence exists to make - that the walking pose is the same value the party carries on foot - is unaffected, and the shrine approach's own terrain is grass, which is not one of the affected ids. `systems/visibility.md` Section 8 owns the table; `catalogs/tile-catalog.md` Section 10 now carries the same caution for the vehicle markers. |
| R510 | issue #294 | `catalogs/tile-catalog.md`, `systems/town-mode.md` | tile-catalog 6 (damage tiles); town-mode 10 (burning family) | The burning family (`0xBC` fireplace, `0x8F` molten lava) is a set of **town** underfoot damage tiles whose handler prints `Burning!`. | Town-only is withdrawn for `0x8F`. The overworld per-turn block has its own lava arm: on both world planes, every turn-consuming action spent on `0x8F` rebuilds the view, prints `Burning!` plus a line feed and rolls `1..8` independently for every non-Dead slot below the party count. Neither the town nor the world arm tests the transport; the on-foot and carpet limit is passability only. `0xBC` stays town-only because it occurs in no world grid. Re-derived 2026-10-12. |
| R511 | issue #295 | `systems/dungeon-mode.md` | 6.6 (class table row `0x6?`; the sentence after the table) | "The normal class-6 rising-pit paint is eligible only when the cell's low three bits are zero", with the table giving the `0x6?` rising entry as "pit when the low three bits are zero". | Class 6 has **no** rising paint. The low-three-bits-zero condition gates the class-6 **floor** pit. A bare `0x62` or any `0x61`..`0x67` draws nothing; `0x60` draws the floor pit only; `0x68` the floor pit plus the rising overlay; `0x69` and `0x6A` only the rising overlay. The separate raw-bit `0x08` overlay has no further condition. Re-derived 2026-10-12 by running the original painter over cell values and bands. |
| R512 | issue #295 | `systems/dungeon-mode.md` | 8 (bomb traps, "fired marker form"), 8.1 | A fired bomb "clears the current trap cell to its fired marker form". | There is no marker form. The cell byte becomes itself masked to bit `0x08`: `0x62` becomes `0x00` (plain passage, draws nothing) and `0x6A` becomes `0x08` (takes the rising overlay). All shipped bombs are plain `0x62`, so a fired bomb draws nothing. Re-derived 2026-10-12. |
| R513 | issue #287 | `systems/magic.md` | Dispel Field helper paragraph (dungeon scenes) | In dungeon scenes Dispel Field "checks the cell in front of the party using the party's facing direction and the current dungeon layer". | It checks the party's **own** cell first and, only when that cell is not in the field family, the cell one step ahead. A field in the party's own cell is therefore cleared in preference to the one in front. The same helper is the Sceptre of Lord British's silent fallback (`systems/inventory.md` Section 7.1). Re-derived 2026-10-12 by static reading. |
| R514 | issue #283 | `systems/view.md` | 4 (class table, "source family" column and the family usage table) | The local view overlay's classes are drawn as "8-by-8 micro-blits" copied from named source banks or families: frame-fill, frame-line, normal terrain, secondary terrain, dedicated rough terrain, a peer/blue family and a blank source. | There are no banks and no tile art. Each "family" is one of the seven user-interface colour slots of `systems/display-driver.md` Section 2 (some biased bright by adding eight), and a micro-blit is a single plotted pixel in the current pen. Per-class colours on EGA and Tandy are now tabulated: frame-fill dark red, frame-line white, water light blue, secondary terrain light green, rough terrain yellow. Re-derived 2026-10-12 by running the original view code over the shipped data. |
| R515 | issue #283 | `systems/view.md` | 4, class `9` row | The hybrid class's two lower-half plots are rooted at `(1,2)` and `(0,3)`. | The plots are at `(2,1)` and `(0,3)`. Re-derived 2026-10-12. |
| R516 | issue #283 | `systems/view.md` | 4, class `0x10` row | The road class draws with the secondary terrain, frame-fill **and frame-line** banks, and its elbow stamp uses "the blank source". | The centre fill and every connection stub use slot 0 (dark red); no frame-line colour is used; only the checker is light green; the elbow stamp is a single pixel of colour 0 (black). Re-derived 2026-10-12. |
| R517 | issue #283 | `systems/view.md` | 4, classes `0xA`..`0xD` and the "Peer/blue terrain" family row | Those classes pick the peer/blue family "under peer/gem view" and the normal terrain family otherwise; "classes that switch banks under peer/gem-view mode". | The tested word is the display adapter identifier, set once at startup, not a view mode. On EGA and Tandy these classes always take the water pen; on CGA and Hercules always the slot-4 pen; the peer/blue pen is never drawn on EGA or Tandy; no Peer, gem or X-Ray colour variant of the local view exists. Same finding as the dungeon renderer's withdrawn peer tint branch. Re-derived 2026-10-12. |
| R518 | issue #291 | `systems/combat.md` | 7 (the paragraph on the panel refresh request's raise sites) | The twenty-three raise sites each follow a change to "spell mana, food, gold, HP from a hazard, or the active-player marker". | No raise site changes spell mana; mana changes only in routines that repaint inline. What a raise follows is HP, member status, food, gold, vehicle marker and hull, marching order, the active-player marker, the timed-effect letter or a found-item counter. The count of twenty-three stands and is now enumerated in `systems/stats-panel.md` Section 2.2.1. Re-derived 2026-10-12. |
| R519 | issue #285 | `systems/combat.md` | 9, morale writer (the classifier's quarter-to-half bucket) | The morale check "sets fleeing on only 4 of 256 possible random-byte results (a roll of 252 or more)" (the R483 text). | R483 fixed the direction of the test but read the draw as a byte. The draw is over the inclusive range `0..256` (257 values), so fleeing is set on 252, 253, 254, 255 and 256: five of 257. Verified by running the original generator over every state. Re-derived 2026-10-12. |
| R520 | issue #284 | `systems/conversation.md`, `formats/tlk.md` | conversation 7.3 (`0x83` and `0x8F` rows); tlk (`0x83` row) | `0x83` PAUSE redraws the world view and "wait[s] for any key", then redraws the party panel for every member, and "if the player presses a designated cancel key, terminate[s] the current stream"; `0x8F` blocks for one keystroke. | PAUSE is a **timed** pause of up to 28 passes (world redraw, non-blocking key poll, one BIOS tick); any key ends it early; type-ahead is discarded on exit; it redraws no party panel (that reading was a misread tick wait); it never terminates the stream and has no cancel key. `0x8F` blocks for one command key, discards it, then continues. Both apply on every response path through the one byte runner. Executed on the original 2026-10-12. |
| R521 | issue #281 | `systems/blackthorn.md` | 3 (captive-cell handoff, last paragraph); 4.2 (the handoff) | "The scene and position are the complete persistent handoff", so the party lands at `(10, 7)` of the Blackthorn's Castle scene on its default floor. | The handoff also writes the floor: floor -1, the castle's basement cell block (floor 0 at that position is the courtyard), and zeroes the carried-key counter. Town entry only reads the floor the handoff left. Re-derived 2026-10-12 and matching the reporter's capture. |
| R522 | issue #296 | `catalogs/quest-graph.md` | 5, shard-use presentation order, step 2 | "It then plays a rising pitch sweep, followed by a falling one, again unconditionally." | It plays the 920-step software-envelope swell of `systems/audio.md` Section 5.4.7: one held pitch of about 937 Hz whose duty cycle rises over 460 runs and falls over 460 more with no gap, 184,000 iterations, about 7.91 s audible. The handler varies only the envelope's comparison value at a fixed phase increment, which changes duty cycle, not pitch. It draws nothing and runs no world tick. Re-derived 2026-10-16 and consistent with the clean side's capture. |
| R523 | issue #296 | `systems/audio.md` | 5.4.7 (swell table row); 8.4 (major-flash callers) | The 2640-increment swell row is labelled "Shadowlord destruction", and 8.4 names Shadowlord destruction as a caller of the shared full-viewport flash that wraps the flash in that swell. | The swell plays on every U-Use of a shard, before the position gate, so the row is relabelled shard use. On the no-effect arm no flash follows; whether a successful destruction invokes the shared flash at all is not established and is indexed in `OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`. Re-derived 2026-10-16. |
| R524 | issue #297 | `systems/commands.md` | 5.2, first paragraph | "Outdoor movement prints its compass word followed by a newline", with no ship exception. | A frigate key that changes the facing prints `Head <Dir>` (plus `Hull weak!` below hull 50) and does not move; under hoisted sail no step, pressed or synthesised, prints the compass word; a furled frigate prints the compass word then `Rowing!`. Re-derived 2026-10-16 by running the shipped movement code across sail state, key, wind, hull and destination; matches both of the issue's captures. |
| R525 | issue #297 | `systems/weather.md` | 5, second paragraph | A heading that differs from the cached sail direction turns the ship without moving it, and movement follows once the key matches the cached heading. | The turn-or-move test is against the ship's facing, not the cache. An unchanged facing attempts the step on that same command in any non-calm wind (calm consumes it silently); this is reachable while the cache is empty (after hoisting, a refusal, or Sheets in irons), and in steady sailing a key equal to the cache is swallowed by the auto-advance loop. Re-derived 2026-10-16. |
| R526 | issue #297 | `systems/text-output.md`, `systems/overworld.md` | text-output 10.2 (Overworld row); overworld 5 step 3 | The prompt-gate flag is cleared by "any direction taken while the party is aboard a ship", including a turn, so exactly one prompt pair is suppressed (R497's replacement text). | It is cleared only under hoisted sail, and a frigate facing change or an under-sail refusal sets it again in the same command, so a course change is followed by an ordinary prompt pair; a furled ship never touches it. R497 traced the clear but missed the two later writes. Re-derived 2026-10-16. |
| R527 | issue #298 | `systems/dungeon-mode.md` | 14.1, post-combat bracket | "Only the **A-Attack** path applies the combat result code"; the contact path "never reads it afterwards, so a wandering-monster fight that begins by being walked into can never change the party's level." | The value is the arena's first-party-exit record (first accepted party-side departure: edges 1-4, in-arena K 5 on an up ladder, 6 on a down ladder or a room grate). A-Attack reads only 5 and 6; the contact path runs the dungeon movement applier on it after the fight, so codes 1-4 step the party one cell (wrapping, no passability test, facing set) and 5/6 change level including both surface exits. Re-derived 2026-10-16 by static reading; not run live. |
| R528 | issue #298 | `systems/dungeon-mode.md` | 14, room cells paragraph | "on resolution the party returns to the room cell with whatever damage and status changes the fight produced." | The party is put back on the room cell and then the same movement applier runs on the first-party-exit record; since a room fight cannot be ended by Escape, a surviving party (apart from the final Doom room's absorption outcome) ends one cell beyond the room cell in the first leaver's direction, facing that way, or one level up or down. Re-derived 2026-10-16 by static reading; not run live. |
| R529 | issue #298 | `systems/dungeon-mode.md` | 8.1, post-action table (Sleep and Poison rows) | Sleep and poison fields play "one short rumble per member whose status actually changes". | One rumble (step 1, target 50, 100..3500 Hz) per member whose save fails, whether or not the status changes: the handler checks only for Dead, so a member already asleep or poisoned is hit again. Sleep also inverts that member's stats row before the rumble and requests a full panel repaint after it; poison has no highlight. Re-derived 2026-10-16 by static reading. |
| R530 | issue #298 | `systems/doors-and-z-transitions.md` | 11, vehicle dismount paragraph | X-Xit "refuses in dungeon/combat-class scenes before testing vehicle state." | X-Xit has no working scene refusal (its dungeon test is dead code) and combat `X` never reaches it; on foot in a dungeon it prints `X-it what?` and, by static reading, reports acted. Confirmed live on the original 2026-10-16. |
| R531 | issue #298 | `systems/conversation.md`, `formats/tlk.md`, `systems/karma.md` | conversation 1 (overview), 7.4, 7.5 (`0x8B` row and prose), 10 ("Curse state"); tlk control-code table (`0x8B` row); karma 4 (dialogue standing census) | "`0x8B` CURSE-CHECK: Run the resident curse-check routine. Used in dialog with cursed-item NPCs (e.g. the snake in Despise) to tick the curse state"; "the curse logic itself owns the per-character byte counters"; the overview's "NPC-curse checks". | `0x8B` (renamed ALARM) runs the town alarm sweep of `systems/town-mode.md` Section 14: forced pursuit for occupied guard, lich/death-mage and Shadowlord slots, one fair `0..255` draw per other occupied slot with a flight rewrite below 128. No curse state or per-character counter is involved; every shipped use sits at a beat where the speaker calls the guards or turns on the party. Re-derived 2026-10-16 by static reading and a shipped `.TLK` census. |
| R532 | issue #298 | `systems/blackthorn.md` | 5, visible sequence and the paragraph after it | The six-step "visible sequence" of every punishing branch: "2. Play the failed-demand cutscene beat. 3. Move one of the two guards and the victim through the audience scene. 4. Apply the roster change ...", and "This five-step sequence ... is the punishing branches' own cutscene", read as applying to the fourth wrong answer's execution. | Two routines. The first wrong answer's reaction runs the failed-challenge script (guard walk, victim onto the pendulum) and removes nobody. The execution (fourth wrong answer or merciful death) runs no cutscene script and moves no guard: record, quiet 10-tick pause, the 920-run swell, the impact presentation at the second-member actor's cell, that actor cleared, the pendulum cell reset to `0x80`, roster removal and repaint, then (wrong arm only) the sliced-in-half page and record `6`. Re-derived 2026-10-16 by static reading. |
| R533 | issue #298 | `systems/active-objects.md` | 8, movement dispatch (whirlpool sentence) | "Whirlpool-class slots flip a stored parity bit and move only on the turns where it clears". | Whirlpool-family slots (`0xEC..0xEF`) toggle bit 0 of record byte 5 on every dispatch and move only when the byte is non-zero afterwards (bit set); on the turn it becomes zero they take no draw and do not move. A spawned whirlpool starts at zero, so it moves on its first dispatched turn and then on every second one. A move turn takes one fair `0..1` draw: 1 runs the directed planner (with its random-walker fallback), 0 runs the random four-direction walker. Re-derived 2026-10-16 by static reading. |
| R534 | issue #298 | `systems/active-objects.md` | 8, Shadow Lord paragraph after the proximity mask table | "Listed cells increment the slot's first auxiliary byte as an age counter; while that counter remains below twenty, the slot requests a directed step toward the player through the same step planner." | On a mask hit the slot increments record byte 5 (wrapping at 256) and then, as on a miss, runs the ordinary directed step planner whatever the counter's value; the comparison with twenty has no behavioural effect because both exits call the same planner and the argument that differs is never read. The branch is taken only for type byte exactly `0xFC`. The increment stays observable through the F-Fire broadside depletion of the same byte. Re-derived 2026-10-16 by static reading. |
| R535 | issue #298 | `systems/active-objects.md` | 3, "The scripts themselves" paragraph | "any value of eight or more sets the frame-delay nibble to that value less eight and advances". | The delay is the value less 128; the shipped values give delays of 2, 4, 7 and 15, and 15 is the freeze sentinel, so a pause-15 step freezes the slot until gameplay releases it. | R536 | issue #298 | `systems/npc-schedules.md` | 8.3 (Phase 4); 10 ("Active-object collisions" and "Player collision") | The dynamic-obstacle overlay stamps "each occupied slot whose Manhattan distance from the NPC's runtime destination is strictly less than four"; Section 10 repeats "Manhattan distance less than four from the NPC's runtime destination" and says "NPCs never step into the player's cell, even if the schedule's waypoint coordinate happens to match." | The overlay walks records 31 down to 1 and stamps only records whose type byte is above `0x3F` (person and creature art; items, vehicles, horses and furniture are not stamped), then the party's cell, each by Manhattan distance under four from the NPC's **current** (x, y). The player is therefore stamped only within that radius; the overlay alone does not keep an NPC out of the player's cell. Re-derived 2026-10-16 by static reading. |
| R537 | issue #298 | `systems/npc-schedules.md` | 10, "Out-of-bounds" | "The 32×32 cell grid is hard-bounded." | The search's bounds test rejects only negative coordinates on the west and north edges and admits index 32 on the south and east edges, so a column of 32 addresses the next row's first workspace cell and a row of 32 reads and stamps the bytes just past the workspace (Section 8.4); the walkability predicate's out-of-bounds rule is as Section 10 item 2 already stated. Re-derived 2026-10-16 by static reading. |
| R538 | issue #298 | `systems/town-mode.md`, `systems/rest-and-camp.md` | town-mode 7 (Hole-up cadence paragraph), 12, 16 ("NPC schedules"); rest-and-camp 4 (compatibility rules) | "For each requested hour the town-hours path can run up to sixteen schedule/world-tick passes, checking after each pass for a rest-interruption event. If an interruption fires, rest stops without rolling back elapsed side effects"; "each requested rest hour can run up to sixteen walker/world-tick passes"; "up to sixteen times per requested rest hour"; "After each interruption check, already-applied time, schedule, and world side effects remain". | One burst of up to sixteen schedule/world-tick passes runs once, at the current hour with no clock advance, after the digit is echoed and before anyone sleeps. An arrest/conflict event (`0x61`) left by any pass ends the command at once with no sleep status, no time passed and no further message, returning the ordinary acted result so the town epilogue re-evaluates contact; a conversation-contact event does not interrupt. The ten-minute elapsed-time loop that follows runs no schedule pass. Re-derived 2026-10-16 by static reading. |
| R539 | issue #298 | `systems/audio.md`, `systems/input.md`, `systems/view.md` | audio 8.3.2 (first "Unresolved" bullet) and 7.3 ("Two non-audio facts" paragraph); input 12 ("first-tick after mode entry" bullet); view, the paragraph on the right-side chrome | The ambient tick "is reached from the world tick only while a mode-entry handshake flag is clear - once after entering a mode, after which the same tick sets the flag"; the wind drift is "gated only on the master-redraw flag and a first-tick handshake flag"; the town-entry hint is read "to decide whether to run the ambient-audio tick"; the world redraw's "first-mode-tick handshake that can repaint the full panel". | The byte is the world tick's ambient/animation gate, normally non-zero. The ambient scenery tick (and the drift and the object animator) runs on every rendering world tick and is skipped only while redraw is disabled, on the first world tick after program start (shipped initial value zero) and on every tick while Negate Time is active (the world tick zeroes the byte at its head). The town-entry hint value skips only the object animator, never the ambient tick, and no world-tick path repaints the panel (`systems/audio.md` Section 8.10). Re-derived 2026-10-16 by static reading and a census of every reference to the byte. |

Cost model: 16,160 checked visits, each about 300 instructions of CPU-bound work (two four-plane pixel copies at sixteen display-adapter port writes per copied pixel, a retune with four speaker-port accesses, one keyboard-status call) plus a calibrated hold of about 55 microseconds. Leave sets the marker and zeroes `+0x17` in the leaving record, then moves that whole record to slot 15 and shifts every later record down one. Pickup moves the guest's whole record to slot party-size, shifts the records between up one, and clears its marker. Every byte (`+0x18`, equipment, stay counter, marker) moves with its character; no byte stays with a slot, and other lodged guests keep their markers. The guest count reads all sixteen markers; the register and its previous/next steps scan records 1-15. An engine that keeps a separate guest table or a marker per slot is wrong. Executed Leave and Pickup with seeded per-record values; static reading of the marker readers. |

Executed from the shipped rescue tail with the real clock routine across twelve starting clocks; the midnight and month side events are static reading. |
