| R589 | issue #300 Q15 | `systems/dungeon-mode.md`; `systems/input.md`; `systems/combat.md`; `systems/shops.md` | dungeon-mode 10 (the dungeon-reader exception), 11 (hours prompt keys), 13.1 (Klimb `U/D` keys and table row); input 5 (the modifier table row, the mark paragraph and the modifier-rule paragraph); combat 8.3 (the second consequence bullet); shops 8.C (guest-register physical keys) | "with Shift or NumLock held the top-row `1`-`4` and `6`-`9` arrive as direction codes and are discarded the same way, while `5`, `0` and Space are still accepted"; "Shifted or NumLock-ed top-row digits stay ordinary digits at the dungeon command prompt and select a solo party member"; "while Shift or NumLock is held, the digits `8` and `2` from the top row too"; "top-row `8` with Shift or NumLock" (dungeon-mode); "digit `1` through `9` while Shift or NumLock is held", "the shifted top-row-digit rule", "an implementation that lacks NumLock state can treat shift held as the trigger" (input); "NumLock on, or either shift held. NumLock on translates top-row and keypad `1`-`4` and `6`-`9` to direction codes, and `5` stays a digit. Shift with NumLock off translates only keypad digits, and keypad `5` stays a digit. | R593 | issue #300 Q9 | `systems/combat.md` | 12, "Splitting / replicating monsters" | "Some classes (slimes, certain gargoyles) carry a "split on damage" flag. When such a monster is *damaged but not killed*, the function looks for an empty slot in the table, copies the parent's class byte into it, and prints `<monster name> divides!`. Up to eight attempts are made to find a free slot." | Only Slime (24) and Gargoyle (30) split, on nonzero, non-instant-kill, non-fatal damage. The up to eight attempts are independent random draws of a cell in the 3x3 block around the parent (off-arena draws redrawn within the attempt), each accepted by the combat-step arena-cell test (so never the parent's own cell; an Energy Field marker blocks, other field markers and corpses do not), then placed in the first free monster slot (6..31); a full monster range fails every attempt. The clone is a fresh hostile of the parent's class with the parent's remaining hit points. `<monster> divides!` has its own line feed and prints only when a clone was placed. Executed original damage application (six scenarios), re-run by a verifier. |

| R596 | issue #300 Q12 | `systems/conversation.md` | 2, step 1 | "**Liveness gate.** A resident "can talk now" predicate decides whether a conversation is allowed at all. If the gate refuses (the most likely reasons are "in combat", "asleep", "starving", or "already in a conversation"), the command returns immediately without printing anything." | There is no liveness gate. Talk's first step is the shared adjacent-direction prompt: a cardinal direction echoes its word and continues; Space echoes `Pass` (row `Talk-Pass`) and ends Talk with the ordinary acted outcome, so the clock advances; Escape, letters, diagonals and unshifted digits are ignored. Nothing in Talk tests provisions, party status, combat or conversation state, so a party with provisions at zero talks normally. An engine that refuses Talk at food 0 locks a starving party out of every conversation. Executed with provisions at 0, 1 and 250 (identical output, provision counter never read), re-run by a verifier; the ignored-digit case and the resident `T` arm reading no food are static reading. |

| R600 | issue #300 Q13, Q21 | `formats/signs-dat.md`; `systems/view.md` | signs-dat 4 (separator and high-bit bullets), 6 (presentation-mode bullet), 8, 9; view 3, the sign token table and the high-bit sentence | "Bytes `0x26` and `0x27` both emit the same separator glyph through the sign presentation mode"; "The high bit controls a presentation mode used by the text output layer; it does not change which low-seven-bit character is emitted"; "Honour the high-bit presentation-mode flag"; view: "the renderer masks it off for output while preserving the path's mode toggle side effect". | The high bit selects the font per byte: clear is runic (slot 1), set is Roman (slot 0). Control tests use the raw byte, so only raw `0x26`/`0x27` (runic `l` bar), raw `0x29..0x31` (runic sixteen-cell macros) and raw `0x0D` (pause, Roman font) are controls; `0xA6`, `0xA7`, `0xA9..0xB1` print Roman characters and `0x8D` is a carriage return. The high bit is removed only when printing an ordinary byte. After the terminator the Roman font is restored and one line feed is printed if the cursor is off column 0. Executed on synthetic and shipped records with the font selector logged, plus a census of the shipped file; re-run by a verifier. |
